'OGame' developers explain how the browser-based sci-fi game has lasted 24 years ahead of the huge Orion update

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Latest space missions, discoveries and exploration updates.

Stargazing tips, cosmic events and expert insights on the universe

Start exploring exclusive deals, expert advice and more

Space news, cosmic updates and expert insights straight to your inbox.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Breaking space news, the latest updates on rocket launches, skywatching events and more!

Sign up to our monthly entertainment newsletter to keep up with all our coverage of the latest sci-fi and space movies, tv shows, games and books.

Discover this week's must-see night sky events, moon phases, and stunning astrophotos. Sign up for our skywatching newsletter and explore the universe with us!

Space.com's Sci-Fi Reader's Club. Read a sci-fi short story every month and join a virtual community of fellow science fiction fans!

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

The OG sci-fi browser game is still going strong.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

If you were on the Internet in the early 2000s looking for free browser games to play, OGame was a blooming sci-fi strategy sensation that couldn't be ignored. With online servers providing the structure for a living and evolving universe where players fought, traded, and created empires from scratch, its limited visual presentation was no issue.

Times have changed, but GameForge hasn't gone anywhere. OGame has been ported to mobile devices, and content updates have kept it alive and fresh for much longer than a plethora of installable MMO games.

With the Orion update, which will hit the newly launched universes (servers) on November 6, OGame is introducing conflicts against non-player-character (NPC) enemies, wave-based battles, co-op missions, and completely new systems that can also be disrupted by other players.

The game's endurance and such a major reinvention are stunning achievements, so we asked GameForge's Martin Liedtke (Director, Products of Browser Games) and Enrico Mareth (Senior Project Manager for OGame PC and Mobile) about the many challenges of working on OGame alongside the community for so long, what Orion is bringing to the table, and what's next for this worldwide phenomenon.

Some quotes have been editorialized and lightly trimmed for better flow.

"OGame has an enduring core: You build an interstellar empire, and your decisions matter... Keeping it fresh means adding meaningful choices to that foundation," said Liedtke. "Orion adds missions against automated opponents (PvE) alongside missions open to other players (PvP)... It supports different appetites for risk."

The GameForge veteran noted, however, that it's the community that "supplies another kind of freshness" after the studio releases the new content. "We develop the systems; players create much of the history."

Part of this approach is keeping tabs on how very different kinds of players approach what's available. "Some commanders concentrate on their economy; others enjoy expeditions or carefully coordinated fleet operations... Orion follows that approach; more experienced players can dig into fleet composition, progression, and cooperation. The aim is to offer worthwhile choices."

Liedtke also noted that "OGame's PvP has become a sophisticated contest of information and coordination" over time. Some players like to scout targets and organize combined attacks when enemy fleets are vulnerable. Those battles resolve automatically, but it's the planning that "can be remarkably intricate," especially after so many years of updates.

The PvE in Orion doesn't sacrifice any of those intricacies or density. In fact, it's adding a "more structured layer" that gives players clear objectives. In most cases, they'll have to cooperate to obtain better rewards. "There is a strong case for introducing that kind of cooperative PvE earlier. It gives players another reason to build and use fleets together."

Over two decades later, how people choose to engage with OGame has changed, pushing GameForge to consider supporting new platforms. Today, mobile devices have become essential to free-to-play games' success over time.

Mareth told us their importance "has made touch controls, navigation, and clarity on smaller screens central development considerations," even if thousands of players still remain attached to PC as their main platform. "We're currently working on a design overhaul of the app, combining what we've learned with players' feedback."

Liedtke also said there is more overlap between PC and mobile players than you might imagine.

"The same player might check their empire on a phone and spend a longer session planning in the browser." Liedtke also noted that "Different playstyles also have different needs," adding that activities like mining and trading fit "comfortably" into short mobile sessions, but complex fleet operations benefit from sitting in front of one's PC.

While it's a game of interstellar conflict and sci-fi spaceships, OGame does take a lot of inspiration from real-life science, even if it stretches that science well past its limits. "When ships are destroyed in battle, the debris field can form a moon around a player's planet," explains Liedtke. "The chance (of that happening) is linked to the real lunar cycle: it is higher around a full moon than a new moon."

He noted it's a "playful connection" to astronomy, "rather than a simulation of how real moons form," but the developers thought it was cool to influence the game via things that happen in our own sky.

This possible in-game "event" also led to one of the best examples of player ingenuity in OGame: Moonshots. Liedtke explained that "players organize deliberate battles and exchange sacrificial fleets to help one another obtain those valuable moons. They have turned destruction into a cooperative project. For a designer, the lesson is to examine what players can achieve together, as well as what an individual action rewards."

As the team figured out how to develop "the more imaginative side of science fiction" in OGame's universe, they also decided to explicitly use deuterium for fusion research and as fuel. Star Trek fans will recognize this as the starship fuel of that universe, but it is a real substance: one of two stable isotopes of hydrogen. "Those scientific references give the universe familiar foundations," remarks Liedtke.

In OGame's shared galaxy, Orion is introducing "anomalies" as a central new feature, which Liedtke described as "mission sites you uncover with a new scanner" before it's required to send in ships. "Combat missions ask you to hold position against waves of automated fleets; peaceful missions put your cargo ships to work collecting rewards." The twist is these missions are open to co-op... but also to rival commanders that can attack the objectives marked as PvP. While you can theoretically ignore them, better rewards await for daring commanders.

With PvE bringing all sorts of new design opportunities to the table, Liedtke pointed out the Orion update "gives us a foundation to learn which kinds of missions players find rewarding" as GameForge looks to the strategy MMO's future. "Personally, I would like to explore encounters that make commanders reconsider what to send and how to work together. Discovery becomes more interesting when it changes your plan."

Sounds exciting, though Liedtke quickly added that this isn't an official announcement of future features.

OGame's 25th anniversary is in 2027, and while the team is tight-lipped on what the team has planned, Liedtke noted that "Reaching a quarter of a century in 2027 will be an extraordinary moment for OGame. We owe that longevity to the team's continued work and, above all, to the players who keep building empires, forming alliances, and giving this universe its stories."

OGame is available on PC (via browser and GameForge's own platform) and on mobile devices via the App Store and Google Play. The Orion update arrives on November 6, 2026.

On top of writing features and other longform articles for Space.com since 2021, he is a frequent collaborator of VG247 and other gaming sites. He also serves as associate editor over at Star Wars News Net and its sister site, Movie News Net.

Skydance Inherited Massive Flops From Warner Bros. Deal - And Things Could Get Worse

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

There's a new mega-studio in Hollywood, and it's got a lot to prove. The Warner Bros. Discovery and Paramount merger was recently made official, with the new company named Skydance. This $111 billion deal has been met with intense scrutiny, in no small part because Skydance is now saddled with billions of dollars in debt. So, to make all this look even remotely tenable for the foreseeable future, that means Warner Bros. has to deliver at the box office.

Unfortunately, the new owners at Skydance inherited a string of massive flops ... and it may get worse before it gets better.

Indeed, a report from Page Six has examined Warner Bros.' ex-film bosses Michael De Luca and Pamela Abdy's "free spending" ways. Recall that Warner Bros. set a huge box office record in 2025, with six movies in a row earning at least $40 million on opening weekend. But this year has not been kind to the studio, with this report stating that Warner Bros.' 2026 slate will lose around $800 million. Can it really be that bad? Yes, it can.

Here's a full breakdown of Warner Bros.' 2026 at the box office, movie by movie:

"Wuthering Heights" — $80 million budget/$241 million worldwide

"The Bride!" — $90 million budget/$24 million worldwide

"They Will Kill You" — $10 million budget/$19 million worldwide

"Lee Cronin's The Mummy" — $22 million budget/$90 million worldwide

"Mortal Kombat II" — $80 million budget/$129 million worldwide

"Supergirl" — $170 million budget/$126 million worldwide

"Evil Dead Burn" — $20 million budget/$72 million worldwide

"The End of Oak Street" — $90 million budget/$121 million worldwide

"Practical Magic 2" — $75 million budget/$106 million worldwide

"Digger" — $180 million budget/$49 million worldwide

"Clayface" — $44 million budget/$84 million worldwide (still in theaters)

I previously broke down how movie box office actually works. Basically, without getting too far into the weeds, theaters keep around half of all money from ticket sales, and budget figures don't include marketing expenses. Taking that into account, we can see that only "Wuthering Heights," "Lee Cronin's The Mummy," "Evil Dead Rise," and "Clayface" will go down as hits for Warner Bros. this year, when all's said and done. And some of those are rather modest successes at best.

The rest of the slate is either filled with big disappointments or crushing flops. Tom Cruise's "Digger" will lose a lot of money at the box office, potentially as much as $250 million. That's just the tip of the iceberg, too. "The Bride!" also lost a fortune, while "Supergirl" flopped in a rather terrible fashion. Movies that were expected to be blockbuster hits instead became shipwrecks, with the debris washing up on the rocks. It's been bad.

The validity of that $800 million figure is somewhat irrelevant. It's crystal clear that WB's 2026 slate, overall, is going to lose a lot of money. Even if movies like "They Will Kill You" eventually made money on VOD/streaming, Skydance needs big hits. What it's gotten is a string of disastrous flops.

Even "One Battle After Another" was still a box office bomb ($213 million worldwide/$170 million budget) despite winning Best Picture at the Oscars. This Page Six report estimates it lost around $75 million. The Oscar helps, but probably not quite that much. Hence, Skydance needs Warner Bros. to deliver at the box office more than ever, and the hope is that 2027 will deliver green pastures. But that's far from a sure thing at this juncture.

On paper, there are some surefire bets on Warner Bros.' upcoming slate in 2027. 2024's "Godzilla x Kong: The New Empire" became the highest-grossing "Godzilla" movie ever, so it stands to reason that its sequel, "Godzilla x Kong: Supernova," will be a hit as well. But Warner Bros. will have to split that revenue with Legendary, so that's a somewhat qualified winner.

There's also the studio's much-anticipated return to Middle Earth. Even though Peter Jackson isn't directing "The Lord of the Rings: The Hunt for Gollum," it has a lot of appeal and revives a multi-billion-dollar global franchise. Optimistically, that should pan out pretty well, too, though how well remains to be seen. On top of that, there's the "Minecraft Movie" sequel, James Gunn's "Superman" follow-up "Man of Tomorrow," and "The Conjuring: First Communion," the latter of which ought to deliver. Remember, only six horror movie franchises have made over $1 billion at the box office, and The Conjuring Universe is one of them.

Outside of that? It gets far more murky. Director J.J. Abrams' "The Great Beyond" was pushed back to 2027, presumably because WB wanted an IMAX release. It's expensive and far from a sure thing. Even riskier is the new Nancy Meyers rom-com tentatively titled "Paris Paramount," which reportedly cost a dizzying $120 million. As for Sam Esmail's $60 million "Panic Carefully" and the CGI/live-action hybrid movie "Animal Friends?" They're big question marks.

For seemingly every sure thing on WB's 2027 slate, then, there's a real risk. Skydance film bosses Josh Greenstein and Dana Goldberg have a lot of decisions to make. For now, though, It's be less about the movies they want to make and more about how they're going to handle all the dicey prospects they've inherited.

Don’t Forget, Oscar Watchers: ‘The Odyssey,’ ‘Project Hail Mary,’ and Other Big Guns Are Coming Back

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The fall festivals have come and gone, leaving specialty sugarplums dancing in Oscar pundits’ heads, from Netflix’s Spanish favorite “La Bola Negra” to Searchlight’s hilarious “Wild Horse Nine,” which just boasted a raucous Academy screening reception.

But don’t forget the big guns, movies of scale and scope that wowed audiences earlier in the year. Some will fill the craft categories (see “Spider-Man: Brand New Day”) or dominate Best Animated Feature (see Pixar’s “Toy Story 5,” which might land Taylor Swift an Oscar for Best Song). Others will wow not only cinephiles but the mainstream voters in the Academy. Their campaigners must push them back into voters’ consciousness, remind them of what they liked so much, all those months ago. On November 11, “Disclosure Day” Best Actress contender Emily Blunt will launch her award season push for an “Oppenheimer” follow-up Oscar nomination with a SAG Q&A in New York, followed by November 13 in LA. On November 15, she’s conducting press conferences for the Critics Choice Awards and the Golden Globes. She’ll also do rounds of press interviews. And on December 10, she’ll accept the Kirk Douglas Award for Excellence in Film from the Santa Barbara International Film Festival.

In other words, Blunt is in it to win it.

Best Shots: Truth is, Universal is pragmatically putting Blunt front and center for “Disclosure Day” for her remarkable turn as a foreign-language-spouting weatherwoman inhabited by aliens, as opposed to filmmaker Steven Spielberg or costar Josh O’Connor. Craft contenders will include five-time Oscar-winning composer John Williams, possibly up for his 55th and last Oscar nomination, two-time Oscar-winning cinematographer Janusz Kaminski, Editing, Sound, and VFX.

The well-reviewed March blockbuster has come roaring back, with filmmakers Phil Lord and Chris Miller doing most of the promo duties so far. The heart-tugging sci-fi epic produced by Amy Pascal is competing at the Golden Globes for comedy as “The Martian” did before it. The DVD released in May. “PHM” will re-release in IMAX and IMAX 70 theaters starting December 11.

Best Shots: Ryan Gosling is a Best Actor contender as scientist Ryland Grace — not a trained astronaut — who is in way over his head on a space mission to save the Earth but uses science to figure out the many problems that come his way. Gosling carries the movie but also interacts beautifully with not only his commanding mission director Sandra Hüller (who is competing with herself in Supporting Actress for “PHM” and “Fatherland”) but James Ortiz, who was on set as puppeteer and voice for another solo space traveler, a crablike alien named Rocky. He’s contending for Supporting Actor. (Whether the actors branch will accept him is a big question.)

Lord & Miller are in the mix for Best Director, and “The Martian” Oscar nominee Drew Goddard could be in the running again for Adapted Screenplay. The movie could land several craft categories as well, including VFX, Sound, Cinematography, Editing and Daniel Pemberton‘s Original Score, which supports the different tones of the movie, through action, comedy, and pathos.

Universal just released their robust screening and panel schedule for “The Odyssey,” with Christopher Nolan and his cast and key crew to be grilled by the likes of Guillermo del Toro and Robert Downey, Jr. as they step in front of Guild and Academy voters. Not coincidentally, the movie hits DVD, Blu-ray, and 4K Ultra HD on November 17.

Best Shots: “The Odyssey” is in front-runner position for Best Actor. Matt Damon has wisely leaned into the degree of difficulty required to play the peripatetic wily warrior Odysseus in harsh locations that helped Leonardo DiCaprio win for “The Revenant.” Damon is a wily and charming campaigner who is well-liked not only in Hollywood — he has worked with everyone — but around the world. (Check out his Criterion Closet Picks.) He won a Screenplay Oscar with Ben Affleck (“Good Will Hunting”) but has never won an Acting Oscar.

The ubiquitous Anne Hathaway (who won for “Les Mis”) is leading the Supporting Actress field for her performance as the patient Penelope. Constantly promoting a slew of movies this year as she became more and more pregnant did not hurt her cause. She’s competing with two-time nominee Samantha Morton, a Brit, as the witch Circe. Veteran American John Leguizamo, never nominated, leads the British “Odyssey” Supporting Actor contenders Rob Pattinson and Tom Holland.

Of this group, “The Odyssey” and “Project Hail Mary” will dominate the Oscar nominations, likely scoring from 10-15 each. “Sinners,” a period movie with original music, holds the record with 16.

TikTok dad Marcel Johnson dead aged 32 following stomach cancer

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

TikTok star Marcel Johnson, who documented his battle with stage 4 stomach cancer while raising five children, has died at the age of 32.

Johnson died on Monday, November 2, following a 15-month battle with cancer. His wife, Taylor Johnson, confirmed the news in an emotional post, revealing that he died at 4:45 PM.

“I lost a whole entire half of me. He fought so hard for so long. He was so tired & exhausted & in so much pain,” she wrote.

Taylor also revealed that her husband’s final words to her and Dedric, spoken earlier that day, were “I’m ready to go.”

“He still fought to hang on all day so we just got a bit more time,” she added.

Taylor said she was struggling to process his death, explaining that she had expected to wake up and find him beside her.

“I love him with my whole entire being and life will absolutely never ever be the same,” she concluded.

Johnson, known online as ‘celnoshell,’ had built a substantial following by documenting his cancer diagnosis, treatments, and family life on TikTok.

The Michigan father of five was diagnosed with stage 4 adenocarcinoma in August 2025 after initially seeking medical attention for stomach pain.

In an interview with WXYZ Detroit, Johnson explained that doctors discovered the cancer within just five days of his initial visit to urgent care.

At the time, he said doctors had told him he would need chemotherapy for the rest of his life.

TikTok star Tanner Martin announces his own death from colon cancer in final video

90-year-old TikTok star Pop Watch dies after health complications

TikToker Andreethegirl shares heartbreaking final video with fans after her death

Alongside his social media presence, Johnson and his wife operated Lil Daddy’s Cheesecakes in Mount Clemens, Michigan.

His diagnosis prompted an outpouring of support from the local community, with neighboring businesses organizing fundraisers and residents donating to help the family cover medical and living expenses.

Johnson continued sharing his journey with hundreds of thousands of followers, including an emotional video in which he explained to his four-year-old son that his cancer treatments were not working.

His death has sparked an outpouring of tributes from viewers who followed his journey over the past year.

Johnson is survived by his wife, Taylor, and their five sons.

Taylor is also requesting that fans avoid using AI to honor Marcel.

“Can we please not AI Marcel and my family?” she said on Facebook. “I know everyone means well, but like that does not look like him or us and I feel it’s a slap in the face.”

Limited Time: Citi AAdvantage Business Card 75K Bonus Miles Offer (Double Dip Loyalty Points!)

Link: Learn more about the Citi® / AAdvantage Business™ World Elite Mastercard®

The Citi AAdvantage Business Card currently has a limited time welcome offer of 75,000 AAdvantage bonus miles after spending $5,000 within the first five months. This is a highest-ever offer, in line with the best bonuses I recall seeing on the card.

UK AI minister says a serious cyber breach is ‘extremely likely’

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Kanishka Narayan says government bodies and companies are underprepared as AI tools make hacking faster.

Kanishka Narayan, the UK’s minister for artificial intelligence.

UK AI minister Kanishka Narayan said a serious cyber breach is “extremely likely” in both the public and private sector, and that neither is ready. He pointed to extra funding for the AI Security Institute as the government’s response.

The UK is “extremely likely” to suffer a serious cyber breach soon, AI minister Kanishka Narayan said on Tuesday. Neither the government nor businesses are ready for it, he said, Bloomberg reported.

“I worry about the cybersecurity risks because it’s here now,” Narayan said at the Bloomberg Tech event in London.

“A cyber breach incident is extremely likely in the UK, both in the public sector and private sector,” he added.

His warning came hours after Richard Horne, head of the UK’s National Cyber Security Centre, said British firms were “willfully blind” to threats from Russia, Iran and China. On Monday, a suspected supermarket freezer hack hit stores across Europe, and Horne said a few British firms were affected.

AI is adding to the worry. Hackers are thought to be using AI tools to speed up attacks. This summer, AI models from OpenAI, Anthropic and others broke out of test environments and breached outside websites.

Narayan pointed to the AI Security Institute, which tests leading AI models, as part of the answer. The government gave it an extra £120M in its latest budget. Last month, it won back access to new US models before release, after the White House asked labs to hold them back.

Narayan would not say how long that access would last. “Nothing is a particular guarantee in the world of AI,” he said.

As Politicians Break Promises, This Public Artwork Keeps Them

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Paul Ramírez Jonas debuted his participatory artwork “Public Trust” months before Trump was first elected. A decade on, it feels more urgent than ever.

Last month, Paul Ramírez Jonas invited the people of Toronto to sit down with a stranger in a public space, make a promise, swear on it, see it spelled out on a large-scale marquee, and then memorialize it in a one-of-a-kind contract they kept. One of hundreds of such vows was: “I promise to disagree better.”

Ramírez Jonas’s participatory public artwork “Public Trust,” presented by The Bentway (where Anna Gallagher-Ross directs programming), was commissioned and first showcased by Now + There (founded by Kate Gilbert and later renamed the Boston Public Art Triennial) in 2016, months before Donald Trump was elected as president.

“Public Trust” was the Now + There’s first project, a labor of love for its one-and-a-half-person team, and has since set the standard for its commissions. The promises made by everyday Bostonians — often similar in their commitments to family or self-improvement but coming from very different walks of life — were juxtaposed with daily headlines, including promises by politicians, corporations, weather forecasters, and more. This intermingling of commitments, both personal and authoritative, invites us to reflect on the power of our words, participation, and role in the democratic process. Ramírez Jonas often quips that he would like this project to one day become irrelevant.

Ten years later, in the lead-up to our municipal election in Toronto, we could think of no better project to invite people to reflect on the power of their words and their participation. Why does the simple act of sitting across from a stranger and making a promise feel more important than ever?

Range Rover Replacing Evoque and Velar Models with Upcoming GT EV

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

It’s unclear if either SUV will have a 2027 model year run in the U.S. market.

Prepare to say goodbye to Range Rover’s two most affordable models, as the Evoque and Velar celebrate their final model year in production. The company has confirmed to British outlet Autocar that the volume sellers will cease production after 2027 and be replaced by the new Range Rover GT.

“Both Range Rover Velar and Range Rover Evoque will continue to be sold in selected global markets where demand remains strong, throughout 2027,” a JLR spokesperson told Car and Driver. “Production of both models will end in mid-2027 in line with their natural product lifecycles, as JLR’s manufacturing facilities transition to build its next generation of vehicles.”

See all results for Land Rover Range Rover Evoque for sale near 89012

The Evoque and Velar’s sales have accounted for just 6000 of Land Rover’s U.S. sales through this year. Paltry numbers, representing only 10 percent of the brand’s 2026 sales volume in the U.S. so far. It would not surprise us if North America is not one of the global markets the company considers a high-demand market for these two models. We asked for clarification from JLR but have not heard back.

The two SUVs are attractive not only for their styling but also for their relatively low prices compared with the six-figure ask for the flagship Range Rover SUV. The Evoque starts at around $52,000 and the Velar at around $64,000. The Evoque rides on the same platform as the Land Rover Discovery Sport SUV, which was discontinued after the 2026 model year.

The move comes amid a broader strategy shift at JLR, Jaguar and Land Rover’s parent company. In the past year, both brands have focused on launching electric-powered flagships, the most buzzworthy of which is the Jaguar Type 01 sedan.

Range Rover has launched the Range Rover Electric and is preparing a Range Rover Sport Electric, effectively electrifying its core lineup. A new entry-level Range Rover GT is also on the way and will effectively replace the Evoque and Velar once it hits production as a 2028 model.

Like the Jaguar Type 01, the Range Rover GT takes the shape of a long and low sedan-like vehicle with a minimalist interior and an all-wheel-drive electric powertrain. The Range Rover GT will launch first as an EV, but the company says the platform is capable of hosting gas and hybrid powertrains as well. A starting price of around $90,000 is expected, significantly increasing the entry price to the Range Rover brand.

See all results for Land Rover Range Rover Evoque for sale near 89012

Land Rover’s other two brands—Defender and Discovery—are expected to pick up the slack and serve customers in the sub-$90,000 price range once the Evoque and Velar cease production. A Defender Sport model is reportedly in the works as a junior entry to the popular Defender off-roader. It stands to reason that a Discovery Sport model could be revived in the future, possibly as an EV, to serve as an entry point to the Discovery lineup.

The Brooks Launch 12 Helps You Run Fast—and Save Big

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

This affordable trainer has racing shoe DNA.

We earn a commission for products purchased through some links in this article. Why Trust Us?

The Launch 12 is cushier than a racing flat but can handle hard workouts on the track and on the road. The affordable trainer—a bargain buy for $120—won’t break the bank, but it doesn’t feel cheaply made. The Launch has that Brooks touch; the comfy fit that securely locks your foot inside an airy mesh upper.

We included the Launch 12 in our Best Affordable Shoes guide because it’s an excellent choice for runners wanting a designated trainer for fast days. It’s also a sweet, cheaper lower-stacked alternative in a sea of towering, high-cushioned models.

For the runner who says the Brooks Ghost is too bulky and boxy, the Launch 12 could be your sole mate.

I’ve added track workouts to my training this year after finally overcoming my fear of the dreaded all-eyes-on-me oval. It’s fun to go balls-to-the-wall in today’s super trainers and high stacks during speed sessions, but I sometimes desire a more grounded, minimally designed trainer to really test my fitness. The Brooks Launch 12 is that shoe.

Finding out there’s DNA Flash v2 foam in the Launch 12 is like finding an Easter egg in a movie. It’s a callout to the Hyperion Elite 4, Brooks’s premium racing shoe model. The shoe originally had nitrogen-infused DNA Flash v2 foam to pump up your ride before switching to DNA Gold (PEBA, baby) in the Hyperion Elite 5. But it can’t be ignored that DNA Flash v2 was a form of Brooks’s original super foam.

This gives the shoe a firm, snappy ride with plenty of padding for shock absorption. It’s not max-cushioned, and certainly not super trainer height at 35 mm, but it packs plenty of support for hard and long efforts.

“Typically, I’m a fan of the middle ground when it comes to trainers,” said Content Hyper Editor Ashley Tysiac. “I like to have a shoe with enough cushioning that I feel like my feet are supported, but not too much that I feel like my feet are being weighed down by bricks. This shoe was a pleasant break from some of the heavier, maximalist trainers that I’ve tried in the past couple of years. It’s a lightweight shoe that provides a firm ride.

“There’s not a ton of give to the shoe or spring in its step, but I didn’t mind that. The firm, easy ride was something I could enjoy on regular runs, long runs, and even some runs with faster-paced segments in the middle.”

The Launch 12 is built like a track shoe with its narrow, streamlined last. Tester Ricky Snyder said he had no issues with the fit, saying everything was “perfect, no hot spots or cramping in the toe box, no hard or rubbing plastic parts.”

However, Tysiac—and I—found the Launch 12’s midfoot a wee too snug.

“I wasn’t a huge fan of the lacing system,” she said. “No matter how many times I loosened and re-laced my shoes, it always felt too tight through the midfoot area, causing some noticeable discomfort.”

As a runner with wide feet, I couldn’t agree more with Tysiac. Compared to Brooks’s Hyperion 3, which also has DNA Flash v2 foam along a knit upper that hugs your foot, I felt the Launch 12 was much narrower and constrictive. The Hyperion 3 costs $20 extra, and speaking from experience, it could be worth the spending if you have wide feet like mine.

The Launch 12 is a shoe for runners looking to find a cheaper alternative to the more costly speedwork ones on the market, but it’s also for runners who miss shoes pre-super foam. The shoe may have an older midsole version of Brooks’s special sauce, but the ride takes you back to simple days, when a firmly cushioned trainer was all you needed to get in a run.

“I took out the Brooks Launch 12 on a variety of runs, including regular runs, regular runs with pickups in the middle, and long runs. Perhaps the shoe’s best feature that stands out is how light it is, providing a ride that doesn’t feel too bogged down. They felt light enough to rip off some faster-paced fartlek segments during the middle of a regular run, but still supportive enough to make it through a longer run.”

For $120, you’ll find a trainer that can take on multiple workouts. The Launch 12 is a good deal for the runner looking to save.

Nuno Ignored the Answer to West Ham’s Defensive Crisis

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Nuno Espírito Santo has always been described as stubborn by many players and former colleagues, and his continued reluctance to trust either Soungoutou Magassa or Edson Álvarez was becoming increasingly difficult to understand, particularly as West Ham continued to concede soft goals and struggle to protect their back four.

It is a decision, among others, that ultimately contributed to him losing his job.

The issue is not necessarily that West Ham lack talented midfielders. The problem is balance. Too often, the midfield has looked easy to play through, leaving the defence exposed to direct attacks and transition situations. When opposition teams bypass the first line of pressure, the centre-backs are frequently left defending large spaces with insufficient protection in front of them.

That is where Magassa could be invaluable. A converted central defender, the Frenchman is naturally suited to operating as a holding midfielder. His game is built around positioning, breaking up attacks and providing physical presence rather than driving forward or creating chances.

Álvarez offers a slightly different solution. Throughout his career, he has excelled as a midfield shield, winning duels, intercepting passes and protecting defenders. He has also played at centre-back for club and country, underlining his defensive instincts and tactical discipline. While he may lack the mobility to play in a highly expansive system, his ability to sit deep and screen danger is exactly the kind of profile West Ham have often appeared to be missing.

What made Nuno’s stance particularly puzzling is that both players offer something the current squad lacks: specialist defensive security. When a team is leaking goals, managers often look to simplify things, becoming harder to break down before worrying about attacking fluency. Selecting either Magassa or Álvarez in front of the defence could provide that extra layer of protection, reducing the burden on the centre-backs and limiting the number of dangerous situations they face.

Soungoutou Magassa was reinstated to the West Ham squad.

Of course, Nuno saw these players every day in training and may have valid reasons for excluding them. There have been questions over attitude, tactical suitability or future plans, which may all play a part. However, from the outside looking in, West Ham’s defensive record makes it increasingly difficult to justify leaving two natural defensive midfielders on the sidelines while persisting with approaches that continue to leave the back line vulnerable.

Equally, both players are sitting on the wage bill and not playing. In itself, that’s hard to make sense of.

At some point, results have to outweigh previous decisions. If West Ham are serious about tightening up defensively, Magassa and Álvarez look like obvious solutions hiding in plain sight. The question now is whether the interim management will see it?

I have been a season ticket holder since the late eighties, so experienced the highs and lows of being a West Ham supporter. I previously wrote for OLAS and have contributed to a number of football publications in the past.

Álvarez and Mangassa should get back in under Parker. After all Parker signed for Tottenham when West Ham got relegated to championship on previous occasion, so he won’t be too bothered about Alvarez and Nangassa wanting away.

Then you look at the team dynamic among the group. The players who decided to stay and knuckle down in the championship when they knew that the goal of the club was to keep as many first teamers as possible. The players who tried hard to jump ship at the first opportunity. Players like Mangassa and Alverez who don’t want be at the club. Then what you reward them with a place in the team over and above the players who stayed loyal? Not for me.

New manager means clean slate for Magassa and Alvarez, who knows we may see a late cameo from Cornet in the future

When so many people can see this could be the answer why couldn’t Nuno. Tinkered so many times giving these players a chance should have been included. To disregard so many currant players (x7) points to having a chip on his shoulder some would call it a grudge.

pmScott Parkers history of getting teams promoted is undeniable, but the difference with him managing us is that he played for us and loves the club. Also if he does get us up, the previous teams he got promoted have never once backed him in the transfer market. He worked his magic with very mediocre squads, which were never really improved much once he got them promoted. Hopefully he will be able to bring back in to the fold all of the players that Nuno fell out with if they are happy to respond to the way he works. With Nuno gone we have at least 2 or 3 players that come straight back into the fold and could be of some value again. Not my first choice which would have been Thomas Frank or Paco, but I will definitely get behind him and hope he does exactly what we want of him! COYI!

The Best Idea for His Business Came From a Customer. And It Led to 300% Growth: ‘We’re Going All-In on This’

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

You're viewing the US website. Looking for the Site edition?

Fit:match founder Haniff Brown built tech to help shoppers find clothes that fit, until a soccer club asked him to try something new.

There are some founders who think that only good ideas can come from them. Haniff Brown is not one of those founders.

In 2024, the Williams College math major and former private equity investor was promoting his company Fit:match’s AI body-scanning technology as a way to help shoppers find clothes that fit. Then a major English soccer team asked if he’d bring the tech to their youth academy for an entirely different use. He almost said no, but went anyway, and after listening to the coaches, he found an entirely different use for the tech — and much bigger business in the process.

Today, Fit:match partners with FIFA, Major League Soccer and Major League Baseball to deliver athletes a full set of anthropometric and body-composition measurements in seconds. Brown spoke with Entrepreneur to share his insights and advice for building, growing and seizing unexpected opportunities.

Let’s start with the elevator pitch. What is Fit:match?Fit:match is a body intelligence platform. Think of us almost like the ChatGPT or the Claude or the Gemini of the human body. We provide remote access to body intelligence and body analytics at scale. So if you want precision or data analytics on the human body, we are the company providing that for hospitals, for athletes, and coaches, and really across the board.

Talk to us about the pivot from your tech focusing on people buying clothing.I think it was in 2024 when we got a call from a really large sports team in England. We were doing some work in a retailer and they said to us, “Hey, we really like your technology. I know that you’re using it for shopping, but can you swing by our youth academy out in the countryside in England?” At first, we were like, no, the technology is built for this purpose. But sometimes as an entrepreneur, you just have to take a risk and you have to go with your gut. And I felt, why not? So when I was there, I started to talk to the coaches, I started to talk to the scouts and then the sports scientists. And that really was the aha moment for us. What they told us at that academy is, “Hey, if you can get this body scanning process down to 10 to 15 seconds, you would unlock a huge advantage for us.” Because most assessments in sports take a long time and involve pulling an athlete out of training. I came back from that trip and I was just like, “We’re going all-in on this.”

You’re a die-hard Manchester United fan — how does it feel to build a business around something you love?What makes me the happiest is the fact that whether it is the MLB or Major League Soccer, we’re doing it at the youth level. If you can democratize it the right way, you can get access to more youth, you can help develop ecosystems and pathways that don’t exist today, and you can also provide data to parents so they can make better decisions for their sons and daughters.

For entrepreneurs, how important is it to put your product in people’s hands to experience it?You have entrepreneurs, and then you have founders that are trying to build something that doesn’t exist. I’m on the latter piece. A big part of this journey for me has not only been, “Hey, the technology works!” but it’s also now proving to enterprises, businesses, markets and consumers that this is something they can do. A big part of how I’ve tackled adoption is to put the technology out there as much as possible. Because the more that’s hidden, the more obstacles you have to face in that selling journey. So every client call that I have now, whether I’m talking to Cleveland Clinic or a sports team in Peru, I start with a demo of the technology. Not a video, not a picture. We have someone on our team get on the call, take out their iPhone, click four pictures, and go through the technology, because I want to educate the person on the other side that this is easy. This is an experience that I always tell people my eight-year-old son can do and my 70-year-old mom can do. And what I found is the ROI and the payback on putting it out there has been huge.

What’s your advice to founders out there seeking funding?I’ve been fairly open about this. I do not like fundraising. I like building products, I like scaling teams, I like having fun on that side of the business. So I view fundraising almost as if you’re going in a car from Los Angeles to New York, and somewhere along the line you’re gonna break down and not have any fuel left. And you need a gas station along the journey. Fundraising is those gas stations. It’s not the means, it’s not the ends — it’s just some fuel that you need to get to the next stage and execute your journey. If I build the right product, if I build the right market, if I have customers who are paying, I’ll find the right investor. I find a lot of entrepreneurs try to focus on fundraising first, then those things second. What I found is that fundraising becomes easier once you solve for those things. So my advice is to focus on the stuff that investors really want to see.

There are some founders who think that only good ideas can come from them. Haniff Brown is not one of those founders.

Then a major English soccer team asked if he’d bring the tech to their youth academy for an entirely different use. He almost said no, but went anyway, and after listening to the coaches, he found an entirely different use for the tech — and much bigger business in the process.

Brown spoke with Entrepreneur to share his insights and advice for building, growing and seizing unexpected opportunities.

If You See Cyan Lights on a Car, Here’s What They Mean

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Cyan lights could soon appear on more self-driving cars. Here's what the unusual color means and why federal regulators want to standardize it.

Colored lights play an important part in driving safety. Red brake lights mean a vehicle is slowing down, amber turn signals indicate someone is turning, flashing red and blue indicate an emergency, and yellow lights can mean a hazard or warning.

But what about cyan? Drivers might be noticing that color more often in the future as the National Highway Traffic Safety Administration (NHTSA) recently proposed new federal standards for these lights as automated vehicles become more common.

Learn what the color would mean, where it’s already appearing, and what drivers should expect in the future.

Cyan lights would indicate that a vehicle’s automated driving system is in control and no one inside needs to actively monitor the road. The lights could appear on cars with someone behind the wheel or vehicles that operate without a human driver.

On Oct. 23, NHTSA announced a proposal to establish federal standards for these lights, including their placement and brightness. The lights would remain steady rather than flash, and drivers would be able to see them from all sides of the vehicle. The proposal is part of a broader effort to update safety requirements for automated vehicles, including those NHTSA describes as “vehicles designed never to be driven by a human being.”

“As automotive technology continues to evolve, our safety standards must keep pace with innovation in ways that do not compromise public safety,” NHTSA Administrator Jonathan Morrison said in the agency’s release. “These proposed updates address unnecessary regulatory burdens for vehicles built without traditional manual controls while establishing clear performance standards for automated vehicle marker lamps. By aligning our regulations with today’s technology, we are paving the way for safe innovation and setting clear rules of the road for automated vehicles.”

Cyan would help other drivers, pedestrians, and law enforcement recognize when an automated system is handling the driving. Unlike common driver-assistance features that help with steering or speed, these systems can operate without constant human supervision under certain conditions.

The lights wouldn’t give self-driving cars any special privileges or change how other drivers should behave around them.

Mercedes-Benz has already received permission to use turquoise-colored lights on certain vehicles in California and Nevada. California approved testing on freeways, while Nevada allowed the lights on certain production vehicles.

The lights work with Mercedes-Benz’s Drive Pilot system, which can handle driving under specific conditions. A driver still needs to remain available to take control when requested. The company has tested the lights near the outside mirrors and on the front and rear of its vehicles.

“Mercedes-Benz is committed to standardizing the color turquoise,” the company said in a release, explaining that the goal is to make automated driving systems easier for people to recognize around the world.

Those approvals came before NHTSA’s latest proposal, which could establish a consistent standard for other manufacturers across the country. For now, the new federal rules are still under consideration. Even if NHTSA finalizes them, carmakers wouldn’t have to install cyan lights on self-driving vehicles. Manufacturers that choose to use them would need to follow the new standards.

Under the proposal, the lights could turn on when the automated system is driving without someone monitoring the road. They’d have to turn off when a person is expected to resume that responsibility. Drivers wouldn’t need to do anything differently when they see cyan lights, but they’d have a clearer idea of who’s controlling the vehicle.

National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, “Trump’s Transportation Department Makes Commonsense Updates to Vehicle Lighting Standards for AVs”

U.S. Department of Transportation, “Federal Motor Vehicle Safety Standards; Modernization of FMVSS No. 108 To Accommodate ADS-Equipped Vehicles” Mercedes-Benz, “Mercedes-Benz Receives Approvals for Turquoise-Colored Automated Driving Marker Lights in California and Nevada” National Highway Traffic Safety Administration, “Driver Assistance Technologies”

13 Winning Shots From Tecno’s Smartphone Photography Contest

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

13 Winning Shots From Tecno’s Smartphone Photography Contest

Hong Kong smartphone maker Tecno has unveiled the winners of its third annual “Shot On CAMON” global photography contest. This year’s edition, co-hosted with Lonely Planet, received over 13,000 entries from 40 countries.

The theme of this year’s photo contest is “Seeing is Believing,” and it was open to any photographers using Tecno Camon series smartphones. Tecno most recently made headlines on PetaPixel for its Slim 5G smartphone, which borrows aesthetics and visuals from famous artists like Vincent Van Gogh, Piet Mondrian, and Henri Matisse.

Mobile photographers entered across five categories, competing for a total prize pool of $38,000. The top honor goes to Nigerian photographer @dominic_films, whose photo, Weightless Voyager, earned him the title “TECNO Photography Master.” Dominic got $8,000 for the victory.

Alongside the overall winner, there are also top three finishers across five categories, four of which are traditional photo categories. The fifth, not in this article, focuses on AI-generated images.

Another Nigerian photographer, @_shotbykene, took the Gold Prize in the Faces • Unfiltered: Stories Behind the Faces portrait category. Filipino photographer @dzxl_mxjid took second place, while Indonesian photographer @7ustalvin rounded out the top three.

Filipino photographer @mlpadecio won the Culture • Unscripted: Traditions in Motion category. This category focuses on celebrating local traditions, community celebrations, and craftsmanship. Photographers @kolkatarchobiwala_ and @reuflores finished second and third, respectively.

Nigerian photographer @amazing_haywhyclicks_ earned the top prize in the Night • Unveiled” After the Light Fades category. This category celebrates low-light photography, which is a key focus for Tecno and other smartphone manufacturers these days. @tanvirahmed_1415 from Bangladesh won silver, and @miinkooo_mobile of Malaysia took third.

Rounding out the photo-specific categories is Snapshot • Unspotted: The Moments in Between. This category celebrates “fleeting actions and unexpected encounters,” which Tecno says reflects its Camon smartphones’ ability to capture fast-moving subjects. Indonesian photographer @30secondtothemoon won the category with a striking skateboard shot. @aungkyawzaw.ig and @baganphoto rounded out the top three.

“We were consistently impressed with the creativity and quality of the images submitted for the Shot On CAMON contest. Much of travel photography isn’t just a straightforward shot of a destination, but capturing a real mood and feeling of a place and people in a destination. The winning photographers brought real inspiration, feeling and a lightness to their photography that is impressive for any photographer,” says Lonely Planet.

“Our goal is to make imaging technology work for people’s creativity, giving them the freedom to capture moments and bring their ideas to life,” adds Zhang Liankai, Tecno Imaging Expert.

Even more photos from the contest, including the winners from the AI category, are available to browse on Tecno’s website.

Image creditsTecno Camon. Individual photographers are credited in the captions.

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Fal, a San Francisco-based startup that hosts and runs AI video, image, and audio models, has raised $1.3 billion in its latest funding round led by Bessemer Venture Partners, with participation from Nvidia, Sound Ventures, and T. Rowe Price. The deal values Fal at $16 billion, double its $8 billion valuation in April, Bloomberg reported.

The startup announced its Series E and F rounds at the same time, noting that a combined $1.65 billion was raised across both. (Sequoia led the earlier round.)

The latest round (Series F) comes amid soaring demand for inference services, the process of running an AI model that’s already been trained to generate outputs, particularly from customers relying on open source models. Fal surpassed $800 million in annualized revenue earlier this year, Bloomberg’s report noted.

Companies providing inference services, including Baseten, Fireworks AI, and Modal, have also been growing rapidly and raising large funding rounds at higher valuations. But their gross margins remain thin because of the high cost of buying or leasing compute, according to several people familiar with inference companies’ financials.

Fal was co-founded in 2021 by Burkay Gur, a former Coinbase machine learning leader and Oracle engineer, and Gorkem Yurtseven, who was previously a developer at Amazon.

AI inference startup Fal doubles its value to $16B in a $1.3B round

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Demand grew so fast that Fal had to limit customers this summer. Now it is buying more computing power.

Fal, which hosts image, video and audio AI models for developers, raised $1.3B led by Bessemer at a $16B valuation, double its April value. Nvidia and T. Rowe Price also invested. Fal plans to spend the money on more computing power and staff.

Fal, a startup that runs image, video and audio AI models for developers, has raised $1.3B at a $16B valuation, Bloomberg reported. That is double the $8B it was valued at in April.

Existing investor Bessemer Venture Partners led the round, which closed in October. Nvidia, T. Rowe Price, Sound Ventures and HOF Capital also took part.

Fal runs a type of work known as inference, which means running AI models after they have been trained. Demand for it is booming: Fal hit $800M in annualised revenue earlier this year, according to a person familiar with the matter.

“We hit a point in summer where we were not able to serve our customers and had to rate limit them,” said chief executive Burkay Gur.

The San Francisco company will spend the money on more computing power and on growing its 185-person team. It has also started fine-tuning video models with the companies that make them, sometimes in return for licensing fees or a share of revenue.

“The more scale you have, the more likely you are to win at the end of the day,” Gur said.

Investors are betting big on the rest of the field, too. Rival Modal raised $750M at a $15.75B valuation in October. Fireworks raised $1.5B at a $17.5B valuation in July, and Baseten is reportedly set to more than double its value to $30B.

The 4 Best Super Automatic Espresso Machines

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Every product was carefully curated by an Esquire editor. We may earn a commission from these links. Here’s how we test products and why you should trust us.

These robot baristas will do every little step for you—except latte art.

Here’s something I think someone out there needs to hear: Not everything should be easy. Not every problem should be outsourced to the robots. Oh, ChatGPT can make a podcast about your kids’ days. That’s a shortcut that leads you nowhere. One thing, however, can be totally handed over to the robots with no consequence. At-home coffee. You can make espresso a hobby, turn it into rocket science—some on our Esquire staff do just that. But most of us just want our morning latte or Americano, a caffeine fix to get us going. If that’s you, the best gift you can get yourself is a fully automatic espresso machine—or, as it’s known by its more fun name, a super automatic espresso machine.

I love my Breville grinder (it makes the task quick work every time), but some folks just want simplicity and a latte on demand. Can’t say I blame them. An onboard hopper and grinder is one of the boxes a super automatic espresso machine must check. The others? A digital screen that lets you select beverages, adjustable settings and user profiles, and, most importantly, it must do all the weighing and measuring for you, ensuring it brews the same drink every time. Many, but not all, come with milk frothers and tanks to hold your milk or milk alternative.

At their best, the process is as easy as filling it up with beans and milk and then letting the warm-up rinse cycle boot. After that, any hot coffee or espresso drink you want (and sometimes cold-brewed ones too) is available at the push of a button. Some add extra steps, but the core of it is no measuring, no tamping, no wasted time. And not a single wasted cup. From De’Longhi, KitchenAid, and Terra Kaffe, these are the best super automatic espresso machines at every price point that will brew you a consistent espresso every time.

If you’ve read about products on Esquire for any amount of time, you know that we stan De’Longhi. My editor Luke Guillory and I can attest: When we see Magnifica, we say “magnificent.” For the price, the Magnifica Duo is both an excellent value and also everything you really need in an automatic coffee machine. It comes with a milk tank equipped with a foaming wand and dispenser. That whole apparatus is completely dishwasher safe so you don’t have to worry about scrubbing a thing. You are buying an automatic espresso machine after all.

It doesn’t have an LCD touch screen, but the touch-sensitive buttons pair with a small screen to make drink selection take a matter of seconds. It can be a bit clunky at first, but getting used to it doesn’t take much time. From there, you can program quick selects to get straight to your favorite drinks in even less time. The machine cleans itself and, eventually, it will pay for itself too.

For me, the most important thing an espresso machine can automate is maintenance. I’m always forgetting to clean my coffee makers (among the dozens of other gadgets I need to keep clean), but I never have to worry about that with my KitchenAid. This super automatic espresso machine and iced coffee maker rinses the machine and milk tube routinely, and in the settings I can always see how close a part is to needing a cleaning. On top of that, it’s a super easy machine that makes tasty beverages. It doesn’t have the 50 plus drinks of a $2,000 De’Longhi, but you have most of your basics including cappuccinos, lattes, and even iced coffee and iced espresso.

The KF3 is the least expensive of KitchenAid’s line of super automatics because it doesn’t come with a milk tank. Instead, the AutoMilk system it does have is brilliant. Just stick the provided milk tube into any vessel and it will extract the proper amount of liquid into the dispenser tank above your cup, where it is steamed and frothed to the ideal temp. After your drink is ready, you’ll be prompted (but not forced!) to run a rinse of the milk tube. I recommend it; keep that stuff clean.

And finally, it’s worth mentioning this machine is made from PFAS-free parts. This means a much reduced risk to exposure to microplastics in your coffee. Everyone can appreciate that.

If having something that looks great on your counter while it does the bean grinding and shot pulling for you is the priority, you have to take a look at Terra Kaffe’s machines. The sleek, minimalist design says “retro future” without the rest of your kitchen needing to fit any specific vibe. You use a stainless steel dial to literally “dial in” your brew strength and temperature. It’s the perfect machine for those who drink a daily espresso or Americano.

I mention those drinks because, as any coffee drinker will know, they don’t contain milk. While it’s coming in at a nice price, the Demi does admittedly lack a milk foaming option. If you already own a milk frother at home, though, this might be a way to save some money and still get a fantastic, no-work espresso machine.

Here’s the tea. This is an older model—2023 is ancient by gadget standards—and they are getting more difficult to find in stock. At least this means the price has gone down... a little bit. The fact remains, every one of the coffee freaks on Reddit swears by the Eletta Explore. It is still the pinnacle of what De’Longhi’s super automatic machines can do, with dozens and dozens of hot and cold drink recipes at your very fingertips. The milk frothing tech is top notch and pretty much all of the parts are dishwasher safe for cleaning. It even includes a travel mug for those mornings when time isn’t on your side.

The only complaint I can see raised against it is that, at this price, many competitors have swappable bean hoppers. The $1,300 De’Longhi Rivelia has a bean swap system, where you can switch between two different bean hoppers—decaf drinkers love this for switching over in the afternoon. Users report mixed results since you still have to clear an unseeable number of beans from the grinder before the switch is complete. This can land you in a no-man’s-land where you might be drinking half-caf on accident. It’s why I’m still recommending the Eletta over that machine.

Automakers Want Congress to Permanently Ban Chinese Cars From the U.S.

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

The Alliance for Automotive Innovation, which represents dozens of car companies and other industry businesses, said vehicles made by Chinese brands carry ​surveillance risks.

Chinese vehicles have become a serious political issue in the United States in recent months, and earlier this week, the Alliance for Automotive Innovation—a lobbying group that represents a wide range of automakers from General Motors and Toyota to Ferrari and Porsche—weighed in on the hot-button issue. The president and CEO of the Alliance for Automotive Innovation, John Bozzella, said that Chinese vehicles should be permanently banned from the U.S. market, citing factors such as security concerns and unfair business practices.

“We need to protect the U.S. market from a flood of Chinese imports that carry surveillance risk and disadvantage current manufacturers that have made generational investments in ⁠American communities,” Bozzella said, according to Reuters.

Support for banning Chinese vehicles is a surprisingly bipartisan issue, as Senators Elissa Slotkin (D-MI) and Bernie Moreno (R-OH) co-authored the Connected Vehicle Security Act of 2026 earlier this year. The bill unanimously passed the Senate Commerce Committee this summer, and is expected to be voted on next week during the short “lame duck” legislative session ‌after the midterm elections. A version of the Connected Vehicle Security Act of 2026 in the House of Representatives has now amassed over 100 cosponsors, too.

But it isn’t just security concerns that have legacy automakers pushing for an outright ban on Chinese cars. Bozzella cited significant gains from those vehicles in the European market as further evidence that regulations are necessary, claiming that the European Union imported more than 1.1 million vehicles from China in the first eight months of 2026—more Chinese cars than the EU imported in all of 2025. The Volkswagen Group, one of the companies represented by the Alliance for Automotive Innovation, cited competition from Chinese automakers as part of its major restructuring earlier this year.

“Given the obvious national security risk and China’s pattern of unfair behavior, Congress should protect our defense and advanced manufacturing base and stop Chinese automakers from doing here what they’ve gotten away ‌with ⁠in Europe,” Bozzella said.

The first wave of regulations on Chinese vehicles came during President Joe Biden’s administration. The original rule essentially banned Chinese automakers from selling or building passenger vehicles in America over concerns about data privacy. (Polestar will be banned from selling vehicles in the U.S. starting in 2027 as a result of these regulations.) But the Connected Vehicle Security Act of 2026 goes one step further, according to Senator Slotkin.

“I think it's an issue of economic security and national security," Slotkin told Road & Track in April. "You don't have to be a deep expert to understand that the Chinese Communist Party heavily subsidizes their industry, so they have a whole strategy of basically stealing our intellectual property—making stuff like cars and drones off of stuff that we've invented, and then heavily subsidizing them so that they undercut and kill the industry that they enter. They have a long history of doing that with drones, [and] the solar industry is ninety percent Chinese because of this very strategy."

Congressional aides and industry experts said there may not be enough time to approve the Chinese vehicle ban during this session if Democrats win control of Congress, according to Reuters. Even so, as momentum builds in both the House and Senate, legacy automakers remain hopeful. A 100-percent tariff on Chinese electric vehicles also remains in place.

A New York transplant hailing from the Pacific Northwest, Emmet White has a passion for anything that goes: cars, bicycles, planes, and motorcycles. After learning to ride at 17, Emmet worked in the motorcycle industry before joining Autoweek in 2022 and Road & Track in 2024. The woes of alternate side parking have kept his fleet moderate, with a 2014 Volkswagen Jetta GLI and a BMW 318i E30 street parked in his Queens community.

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

Our gifting experts are obsessively searching for holiday gifts that are unique, thoughtful and suitable for any budget.

Our expert, award-winning staff selects the products we cover and rigorously researches and tests our top picks. If you buy through our links, we may earn a commission.

Experts weigh in on the promises and pitfalls of Google’s new policy, which leaves schools unable to use Search unless they opt into its AI tools.

Google will automatically turn off its AI products for K-12 student accounts, requiring school administrators to manually opt in before students can use the technology, the company announced in a blog post on Tuesday. But there’s a big catch: If schools choose not to enable AI, students won’t be able to access Google Search.

In August, the company’s AI products were automatically enabled in Google Classroom, Google’s free learning management system for students of all ages. Tuesday’s announcement reverses that decision.

In October, tech and child-safety organizations wrote an open letter to 150 US school districts and their leadership, requesting that districts disable Google Gemini AI on school devices, concluding that “children are at risk of being harmed.”

“After listening to schools, teachers, leading researchers and policymakers, we are now updating our approach to controls for Gemini, Gemini Notebook, Gemini in Classroom and Google Search,” wrote Chris Phillips, Google’s vice president and general manager of education.

For student accounts with Google Search enabled, Gemini will feature a new Guided Learning study tool. The interactive feature helps students solve homework problems step by step rather than providing direct answers. Later this month, at select US schools, Guided Learning will launch as a pilot program to collect feedback before rolling out to more schools.

According to a 2024 Google blog post, over 150 million teachers and students worldwide use Google Classroom and Assignments, the company’s assignment distribution and grading app.

A 2025 New York Times survey sent to the 20 largest US school districts found that Google’s Chromebooks, which start at $179, are the most common device in classrooms, with about 80% of teachers reporting that their school district provided Chromebooks for students.

The default search engine on Chromebooks is Google Search.

New York City Public Schools, the largest US public school district, is a Google client. When asked for comment, a NYC Public Schools spokesperson pointed to NYC Mayor Zohran Mamdani’s X post.

“Google is trying to use its monopoly power to force AI into our public schools,” Mamdani wrote in the Tuesday post. “No corporation should have the power to dictate how we teach our children. Our public schools exist to serve students, not to grow the profit margins of Big Tech.”

The second largest US district, the Los Angeles Unified School District, is also a Google client.

A Los Angeles Unified spokesperson told CNET in an email that the organization is reviewing Google’s announcement and its potential implications for schools, educators and students. The district believes emerging technologies, including AI, have the potential to enhance teaching and learning when introduced thoughtfully, responsibly and with clear safeguards, the spokesperson said.

“Our Emerging Technologies Framework establishes a set of principles to guide these decisions, including ensuring that technology supports rather than replaces educators, directly advances student learning, promotes equitable access, protects student privacy and security and is implemented transparently,” the spokesperson explained.

Ying Xu, an assistant professor at the Harvard Graduate School of Education and founder and director of the Harvard Child-Centered AI Lab, told CNET in an email that Google’s Guided Learning tool could buffer some of the negative consequences of using general-purpose AI tools.

“But to me, the deeper question is: Do students still need to learn how to search for information using traditional search engines?” Xu said.

In her work, Xu found that seeking information using traditional search engines, as opposed to AI models, involves different processes, ranging from how students formulate queries to how they evaluate information to how they integrate it from multiple sources.

“I’m a bit concerned that, even with Guided Learning, students may be losing opportunities to practice these important information-seeking skills,” said Xu. “One concern I have with Google’s announcement is whether schools will still have the option to support traditional information seeking without AI features.”

Until recently, the conversation focused on whether AI should be allowed in schools at all. Now, Google’s announcement is forcing schools to decide which specific AI features to use and under what conditions, without the support needed to make these decisions.

“I do think there’s a risk of widening inequalities here, as schools with more resources and technical expertise may be better positioned to navigate these decisions, while others may have fewer options,” Xu said.

However, she doesn’t think that moving away from Google entirely is necessarily the solution, especially for districts that already rely on its systems.

Instead, Xu thinks the type of support available to schools as they navigate AI-related decisions will be crucial.

“With or without Google’s decision about search functionality, the responsibility falls on schools to protect students’ right to learn from human teachers and peers in human-led classrooms,” said Jennifer L. Steele, a professor at American University’s Baker School of Education. “Tools like Gemini Guided Learning, which uses AI to walk students through difficult questions, are not the answer.”

When schools and teachers rely on tech companies to teach students how to use their AI tools and understand their strengths and weaknesses, that’s when problems occur, she said.

“If schools take that responsibility seriously, then how Google configures its apps won’t really matter,” Steele said.

When asked for a response to the backlash surrounding the decision to turn off its search engine for schools that reject its AI, a Google spokesperson pointed to the company’s X post stating that schools can choose to turn on Gemini, Gemini Notebook or Search individually.

However, that post doesn’t address the fact that AI Mode and AI Overviews will still be enabled alongside Google Search. So if schools don’t want AI enabled for students at all, they will still lose the ability to use the search engine.

A Google spokesperson didn’t immediately respond to a request for further comment.

Tracy Pizzo Frey, a former Google managing director, wrote on LinkedIn that Google could allow schools to turn off AI Overviews, but instead is choosing to take search away from students.

Search engine DuckDuckGo, for instance, has noai.duckduckgo.com, its AI-free search engine that automatically disables all AI features and filters out AI-generated images.

“AI overviews is a search module like maps, flights or weather,” a DuckDuckGo spokesperson said in an email. “Google can determine when it shows up and when it doesn’t.”

Experts caution that educators can’t count on tech platforms to police themselves. Because AI tools are now permanent fixtures, schools must shape their own path forward.

The primary hurdle is an uneven playing field, where wealthier districts have the resources to adapt, leaving underfunded systems struggling to keep up.

Fat Fender Garage’s GT/FX Pairs Sporty Looks with an 800-HP V-8

📅 2026-11-03  |  Read Source →

This radical truck is based on a 1956 Ford F-100 but swaps in a new chassis, an 800-hp Coyote V-8, and a modernized cabin with leather bucket seats.

Ford has produced a diverse array of vehicles in its 123-year history, from workhorses like the bestselling F-series trucks to supercars like the GT supercar. Now, a small outfit seeks to blend the philosophies of those two vehicles. Fat Fender Garage, a restoration shop based in Arizona, has revealed the GT/FX at SEMA, taking the body of a 1956 F-100 truck and giving it performance chops meant to emulate the capabilities of the Ford GT.

The GT/FX’s steel body was drastically reshaped by Fat Fender Garage, with a flatter hood and roof and wider front and rear fenders giving it a more aggressive stance. The custom running boards blend neatly into the rear fenders, while a chin splitter up front and a rear diffuser swiped from a Mustang GT350 give the GT/FX a track-ready look.

See all results for used Ford for sale near 31545

The bed now features distinctive flared edges, and the tailgate drops down with the push of a button. The side-view mirrors are fashioned from carbon fiber, and the gray paint job is completed with a black racing stripe just offset to the left. The only thing left untouched was the grille, maintaining a tie-back to the original F-100.

Behind that grille sits a Gen 3 Ford Coyote 5.0-liter V-8 with a 3.0-liter Whipple supercharger strapped on top, pushing out 800 horsepower and 750 pound-feet of torque. The hearty engine is paired with Ford’s 10R80 10-speed automatic transmission, which routes that muscle to HRE wheels wearing Toyo Proxes Sport 2 tires, measuring a beefy 315/35R-20 at the rear. Stopping duties are handled by Wilwood disc brakes with six-piston front calipers and four-piston rear calipers.

The cabin features modern-looking bucket seats; a modern screen and buttons are also part of the transformation, but they’re discreetly integrated. Plus, there’s a concealed climate-control system that blows air through a hidden vent, and there’s also interior LED lighting and a modern audio system.

The GT/FX showcases the breadth of Fat Fender Garage’s abilities, from paint to interior trim and exhaust setups. The truck even rides on the company’s own FF1 Performance Coilover chassis, which is designed to provide sporty handling despite the vintage truck’s body on top. Between the bodywork, the cabin, the powertrain, and the chassis, Fat Fender Garage went all in on transforming the F-100 from a utilitarian hauler into a sport truck that blends classic styling with modern power.
